    "type": "skill_listing",
    "content": "- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Also handles migrating existing Claude API code between Claude model versions (4.5 → 4.6, 4.6 → 4.7, retired-model replacements).\nTRIGGER when: code imports `anthropic`/`@anthropic-ai/sdk`; user asks for the Claude API, Anthropic SDK, or Managed Agents; user adds/modifies/tunes a Claude feature (caching, thinking, compaction, tool use, batch, files, citations, memory) or model (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku) in a file; questions about prompt caching / cache hit rate in an Anthropic SDK project.\nSKIP: file imports `openai`/other-provider SDK, filename like `*-openai.py`/`*-generic.py`, provider-neutral code, general programming/ML.\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a pull request\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch",

mean_surprisal (0.089 vs 0.088)\\n5. p75_ppl (0.069 vs 0.067)\\n\\n### Code contamination\\n| Depth | Baseline | Temp09 | Δ |\\n|-------|----------|--------|---|\\n| d1 | 5.0% | 7.7% | +2.7pp |\\n| d2 | 9.0% | 11.6% | +2.6pp |\\n| d3 | 12.0% | 13.6% | +1.6pp |\\n\\n### Analysis\\n1. **Depth signal robust across decoding strategies**: temp_only τ=0.9 achieves 76.25% 4-class acc (vs 78.15% baseline), only 1.9pp lower — well within the hypothesis that depth signal is decoding-strategy-agnostic.\\n2. **Ordinal structure preserved**: AUC ordering 0v1 > 1v3 > 1v2 > 2v3 identical to baseline, confirming the difficulty gradient d0≫d1>d2>d3 is robust.\\n3. **d2 remains the weak link**: d2 recall (0.554 in 3-class) is the lowest, consistent with baseline — d2 is the hardest to distinguish from both d1 and d3.\\n4. **Feature importance stable**: Top-5 features identical between strategies with near-identical importance weights, suggesting the same statistical signatures carry depth information regardless of decoding method.\\n5. **Higher code contamination**: Temp09 shows 2-3pp higher code-like content across depths, possibly because temperature-only sampling without top-p truncation preserves more of the distribution tail including code patterns. The filtered 3-class acc (72.52%) mitigates this.\\n6. **Conclusion: SUCCESS** — 4-class acc 76.25% >> 40% threshold, d1v2 AUC 0.8327 >> 0.6 threshold. across decoding strategies**: temp_only τ=0.9 achieves 76.25% 4-class acc (vs 78.15% baseline), only 1.9pp lower — well within the hypothesis that depth signal is decoding-strategy-agnostic.\\\\n2. **Ordinal structure preserved**: AUC ordering 0v1 > 1v3 > 1v2 > 2v3 identical to baseline, confirming the difficulty gradient d0≫d1>d2>d3 is robust.\\\\n3. **d2 remains the weak link**: d2 recall (0.554 in 3-class) is the lowest, consistent with baseline — d2 is the hardest to distinguish from both d1 and d3.\\\\n4. **Feature importance stable**: Top-5 features identical between strategies with near-identical importance weights, suggesting the same statistical signatures carry depth information regardless of decoding method.\\\\n5. **Higher code contamination**: Temp09 shows 2-3pp higher code-like content across depths, possibly because temperature-only sampling without top-p truncation preserves more of the distribution tail including code patterns. The filtered 3-class acc (72.52%) mitigates this.\\\\n6. **Conclusion: SUCCESS** — 4-class acc 76.25% >> 40% threshold, d1v2 AUC 0.8327 >> 0.6 threshold. GPU UUID mapping:
- GPU 0 (cuda:0): GPU-c21cac7d → PID 211344, 15620 MiB
- GPU 1 (cuda:1): GPU-bc769c55 → PID 207537, 9964 MiB

Registry says:
- gpt2xl_topk50_001: cuda:0, PID 211326 (but PID 211344 is on GPU 0 — the PID might be a child process of the tmux session)


- olmo_temp09_001: cuda:1 (no PID recorded in spec but it's running)

The PID discrepancy makes sense — 211326 is the session PID recorded at submission, while 211344 is the actual Python process spawned from it. Both experiments are running on their assigned GPUs within the project's allocation, so there's no violation. Let me check the experiment logs to see how they're progressing, and then I'll review the coverage matrix issue with plan_002b and plan_003.

Looking at the GPU metrics, both processes are active and properly allocated — cuda:0 is running GPT-2 XL at 16% memory and 63% compute, while cuda:1 is running OLMo-1B at 10% memory and 68% compute. The low memory usage is expected given the card sizes, and neither GPU is idle. The real issue is that plan_002b and plan_003c are showing as "planned" in the coverage matrix because they're missing the registry_id link, even though they have active running experiments.

The experiment_plan.yaml is stale — it hasn't been updated to reflect that these experiments are now running under olmo_temp09_001 and gpt2xl_topk50_001, which is why the coverage percentage is undercounting at 31.8% when actual coverage is higher. I've also verified the D007 feature importance claim: var_surprisal ranks first across all Pythia strategies and second in most other settings, while p90_ppl consistently ranks in the top 2. For the DCOL scheduling, dcol_deberta_003 is queued correctly on cuda:0 after gpt2xl_topk50_001 completes.

There's a tracking gap between the ROADMAP (which marks "Atlas v1 完成") and the experiment plan (which still lists plan_006 as "planned") — the Atlas analysis was done informally using completed cells but never registered as a formal experiment. The strategic pivot from DCOL-primary to Atlas/EDD-primary makes sense given DCOL's two failures and the strong results from the RF and statistical features approach, which also provides good risk coverage if DCOL v3 fails. Now I'm looking at the top-k signal attenuation issue.

Pythia's topk50 shows an 8.6 percentage point drop compared to nucleus sampling, which aligns with the ROADMAP's observation about top-k signal degradation, but I need to wait for the GPT-2 XL results to determine if this is model-specific or a broader pattern. The budget looks solid at 14 GPU hours consumed with 19 remaining, which is manageable over the next half day on two GPUs. The main thing to address before writing is that experiment tracking gap in the YAML file.
